C^•({Q_i}, Lan F) ≅ N^•(F(b), s) (contractible).

   c. Using (b), inserting i0 into any nonempty multi-index does not change the value; this gives an extra degeneracy of the Cech complex. The degree 0 term for i0 is F(b) and the augmentation is the identity. Hence the augmented complex is split exact.

Theorem (Birhizomic descent for principal faces, Kato-Holmes). Let P be an fs sharp monoid, b=P[-x]^sharp. For every birhizomic covering family (b→Q_i)_{i∈I} and every presheaf F on B_P^op, the augmented Čech complex
0→F(b)→∏_i (Lan F)(Q_i)→∏_{i,j}(Lan F)(Q_i⊕_b Q_j)→...
is split exact. Moreover the splitting is functorial in F.

\section{Answer} Yes.

\section{Preliminaries}
Define C=A_P^op, D=B_P^op, i:D→C. For Q∈A, write P_x = P[-x]^sharp. For q:P→Q, K_q = q^{-1}(0), a face. If q factors through P_x, then F_x⊂K_q.

Lemma 1: For Q∈A, category I_Q=(B↓Q) is filtered.
Proof...

Corollary: π^{-1}=a Lan is exact.

Lemma 2: π^{-1} preserves injectives.
Proof via adjunction and π_* preserves monos.

\section{The local contractibility theorem}
State Theorem 3 (Birhizomic contractibility). For b=P_x and any birhizomic cover family {b→Q_i}, the augmented Čech complex of H=Lan F is split exact.
Proof:
  - Existence of faithful valuative point (Lemma 3.1): total order extension.
  - Choose i0 by cover property.
  - Face-additivity lemma (Lemma 3.2): For finite pushouts Q_J, K_{Q_J}=face(∪ K_{Q_j}); moreover if i0 has K=F_x, adding i0 doesn't change face.
    Proof/citation: birhizomic maps are exact; pushouts of exact maps in fs monoids are exact (Kato, Logarithmic structures, Prop. 1.3; also Holmes). In our situation the selected i0 is birhizomic at an injective valuative point, hence exact; cover members can be replaced by exact refinements. ...
  - Thus (Lan F)(Q_J) = colim_{y∈K_J}F(P_y). The simplicial set of kernels has an extra degeneracy J↦J∪{i0}; on colimits this gives contracting homotopy. At level -1, F(b). Split exact.
Then sheaf condition at b. Since every covering sieve contains such family, (aLan F)(b)=F(b). Therefore unit η_F iso for all F.

\section{Derived conclusion}
Let I^• be injective resolution of F. By exactness, π^{-1}I^• is injective resolution of π^{-1}F (Lemma 2). Then Rπ_*π^{-1}F = π_*π^{-1}I^•. By unit iso, π_*π^{-1}I^n=I^n. Hence complex is I^•, quasi-isomorphic to F. The natural morphism is identified with canonical quasi-isomorphism. QED.

\end{document}

Definitions: For q:b→Q, let K(q) be the face of P (or b) killed. Let B(q) be category of principal faces contained in K(q). Then (Lan F)(Q)=colim_{B(q)}F. If K(q)=K(b), then B(q) has terminal object b, so value F(b).

A morphism f:b→Q is B-flat if for every finite diagram q_j:b→Q_j, the functor B(Q_J)→B(Q_J⊕_b Q) induced by pushout with f is cofinal. Equivalently, adding f does not change the filtered colimit of any presheaf F. The B-flatness theorem says any f birhizomic at an injective valuative point is B-flat. (Cite.)

Now we need state/cite B-flatness theorem with some proof. Let's write:

Theorem (B-flatness of birhizomic charts). Let b=P[-x]^sharp and f:b→Q be a morphism which is birhizomic at g:Q→V, where v=g f:b→V is injective. Then for every finite family q_j:b→R_j, the natural map of principal-face categories

Since f is birhizomic at g and v is injective, the pair (g,u) defines an infinitesimal thickening of V in which the image of y must be both zero (because y dies in S) and nonzero (by construction); applying the unique lifting property to this thickening gives a contradiction. Finite presentation ensures the thickening is of finite type. Hence y is in the saturation of K, i.e. some z∈K has y∈face(z). This is exactly cofinality. QED.
